## P4 - Clarify the `errInterfaceRemoved` doc comment in [`capture/local.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/ea9c5e8bd08d161744c213960668476a15e35d36/capture/local.go#L1-L1)

**Location:** [capture/local.go:50-51](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/ea9c5e8bd08d161744c213960668476a15e35d36/capture/local.go#L50-L51)

**Problem:** The comment added by this PR reads&#58; &#34;`errInterfaceRemoved` is why an interface that was back by the first `reopen` was gone.&#34; The phrase &#34;back by the first `reopen`&#34; reads like &#34;restored by&#34; rather than &#34;back before&#34;, and the sentence does not say what the error is used for. The explanation only becomes clear after reading the `reopen` doc comment and the probe comment further down the file.

**Trigger:** A reader of [`capture/local.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/ea9c5e8bd08d161744c213960668476a15e35d36/capture/local.go#L1-L1) encountering the error declaration before reading `reopen`.

**Impact:** Small maintenance cost only; the code behavior is correct. The declaration does not explain itself where it sits.

**Suggested correction:** Reword the comment to state purpose and trigger, for example&#58; &#34;`errInterfaceRemoved` reports that the interface left the network namespace of the failed socket, which a restart that is already over when the first `reopen` attempt runs does.&#34; The assembled gap reason &#40;&#34;the interface was not available&#58; it was removed from its network namespace&#34;&#41; is acceptable as is.


## Coverage

- Both changed paths &#40;[`capture/local.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/ea9c5e8bd08d161744c213960668476a15e35d36/capture/local.go#L1-L1), [`capture/local_test.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/ea9c5e8bd08d161744c213960668476a15e35d36/capture/local_test.go#L1-L1)&#41; were reviewed in full against the base revision, including all diff hunks and surrounding code.
- Traced all implementers of the changed `packetSource` interface&#58; only `afpacketSource` and the test `fakeSource` implement it, both updated. Verified close-once ownership across run, `reopen`, `openAll`, and the `NewOutput` failure path; no double-close or fd leak found, and the probe fd is closed on its own error paths.
- Verified gap-reporting orderings&#58; gone&#40;&#41; and thus Removed&#40;&#41; is evaluated before defer `failed.Close`&#40;&#41; runs in both exit paths, so the probe socket is alive when consulted; the new test&#39;s synchronization &#40;close observation, then mutex-guarded gap read&#41; is sound.
- Docs&#58; [`docs/cmd/capture.md`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/ea9c5e8bd08d161744c213960668476a15e35d36/docs/cmd/capture.md#L1-L1) line 95 and [`docs/rn/0.83.md`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/ea9c5e8bd08d161744c213960668476a15e35d36/docs/rn/0.83.md#L1-L1) line 43 already document that the time an interface is gone is reported as a warning; this PR closes the implementation gap for fast restarts, so no doc edit is required. No topology API change, so [`schemas/clab.schema.json`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/ea9c5e8bd08d161744c213960668476a15e35d36/schemas/clab.schema.json#L1-L1) is unaffected.
- The kernel-side claim behind the probe &#40;an `AF_PACKET` socket&#39;s bound ifindex read back via getsockname becomes -1 when its device is unregistered, deleted, or moved, and stays unchanged on mere link-down&#41; matches the PR comment and is consistent with `net/packet/af_packet.c` notifier behavior, but could not be verified against kernel source in this environment; the new unit test exercises the Go logic with a fake source rather than the kernel path. The base code already relied on the related assumption that a removed interface&#39;s socket reports an error.
- Resource note, not a defect&#58; the failed source &#40;8 `MiB` ring plus probe fd&#41; is now held for the whole `reopen` wait instead of being closed immediately at error time. This is inherent to consulting Removed&#40;&#41; at `reopen` end, and the cost is bounded by the number of capture targets.
- Tests were not executed &#40;source-only review&#41;; formatting was inspected by reading and appears gofmt-clean, with no supplied CI evidence to cite.

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

If uncertain, ask.\n- If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.\n- If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.",

        "type": "file",
        "path": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc",
        "text": "---\ndescription: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Karpathy behavioral guidelines\n\nBehavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.\n\n**Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.\n\n## 1. Think Before Coding\n\n**Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**\n\nBefore implementing:\n\n- State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.\n- If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.\n- If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.\n- If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.\n\n## 2. Simplicity First\n\n**Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**\n\n- No features beyond what was asked.\n- No abstractions for single-use code.\n- No \"flexibility\" or \"configurability\" that wasn't requested.\n- No error handling for impossible scenarios.\n- If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.\n\nAsk yourself: \"Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?\" If yes, simplify.\n\n## 3. Surgical Changes\n\n**Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**\n\nWhen editing existing code:\n\n- Don't \"improve\" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.\n- Don't refactor things that aren't broken.\n- Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.\n- If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.\n\nWhen your changes create orphans:\n\n- Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.\n- Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.\n\nThe test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.\n\n## 4. Goal-Driven Execution\n\n**Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**\n\nTransform tasks into verifiable goals:\n\n- \"Add validation\" → \"Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass\"\n- \"Fix the bug\" → \"Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass\"\n- \"Refactor X\" → \"Ensure tests pass before and after\"\n\nFor multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:\n\n```\n1. [Step] → verify: [check]\n2. [Step] → verify: [check]\n3. [Step] → verify: [check]\n```\n\nStrong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria (\"make it work\") require constant clarification.\n\n---\n\n**These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.",

<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

21: const (
22: 	// localReorderWindow is how long the merged stream holds packets of local sockets back to
23: 	// write them in timestamp order. Each socket hands over its packets within
24: 	// localBlockTimeout, so a few times that covers the delivery skew between sockets.
25: 	localReorderWindow = 100 * time.Millisecond
26: 	// localBlockTimeout is how long the kernel fills a ring block before it hands it over.
27: 	localBlockTimeout = 10 * time.Millisecond
28: 	// localPollTimeout is how often a socket reader checks whether the capture ended.
29: 	localPollTimeout = 100 * time.Millisecond
30: 	// localReopenInterval is how often a reader tries to reopen the socket of an interface
31: 	// that went down or disappeared.
32: 	localReopenInterval = 200 * time.Millisecond
33: 	// localDrainQuiet is how long after the end of a capture a reader keeps waiting for packets
34: 	// still in the socket's ring: the kernel hands over a block within two block timeouts.
35: 	localDrainQuiet = 5 * localBlockTimeout
36: 	// localDrainTimeout bounds the drain while packets keep arriving.
37: 	localDrainTimeout = 250 * time.Millisecond
38: 	// localRingBlocks and localMinBlockSize size the receive ring of each socket: eight blocks
39: 	// of at least 1 MiB, each larger than the snap length.
40: 	localRingBlocks   = 8
41: 	localMinBlockSize = 1 << 20

69: 	// returns errReadTimeout when no packet arrived within the poll timeout.

85: // CaptureLocal streams pcapng from interfaces of nodes on this machine to w, with the same
86: // output and end conditions as labruntime.PacketCapturer.Capture: one section, one Interface
87: // Description Block per target named <node>:<interface>, then the packets of every target in
88: // timestamp order. targets lists the interfaces of req.Targets, resolved, in the same order.
89: //
90: // Each interface is read through a packet socket opened in the node's network namespace, so
91: // nothing runs in the node and the node image needs no capture tools; it needs CAP_NET_RAW and
92: // the right to enter the namespace, so root. An interface that goes down is reopened once it is
93: // up again; one that disappears (its node stopped or was recreated) is reattached when it is
94: // back, and the time it was gone is reported as a gap through req.OnGap (or logged).
95: func CaptureLocal(
96: 	ctx context.Context,
97: 	req clablabruntime.CaptureRequest,
98: 	targets []LocalTarget,
99: 	w io.Writer,
100: ) error {
101: 	return localCapture{
102: 		open:           openPacketSockets,
103: 		window:         localReorderWindow,
104: 		reopenInterval: localReopenInterval,
105: 	}.run(ctx, req, targets, w)
106: }

108: // localCapture is a local capture with its socket opener and timings, which tests replace.
109: type localCapture struct {
110: 	open           openSourcesFunc
111: 	window         time.Duration
112: 	reopenInterval time.Duration
113: }

115: func (l localCapture) run(
116: 	ctx context.Context,
117: 	req clablabruntime.CaptureRequest,
118: 	targets []LocalTarget,
119: 	w io.Writer,
120: ) error {
121: 	if err := ValidateRequest(&req); err != nil {
122: 		return err
123: 	}
124: 	if len(targets) != len(req.Targets) {
125: 		return fmt.Errorf("capture has %d resolved targets for %d requested ones",
126: 			len(targets), len(req.Targets))
127: 	}
128: 
129: 	sources, err := l.openAll(ctx, targets, req.SnapLength)
130: 	if err != nil {
131: 		return err
132: 	}
133: 
134: 	names := make([]string, len(targets))
135: 	for idx, target := range targets {
136: 		names[idx] = target.Node + ":" + target.Interface
137: 	}
138: 
139: 	captureCtx, stop := context.WithCancel(ctx)
140: 	defer stop()
141: 	if req.Duration > 0 {
142: 		var cancelTimeout context.CancelFunc
143: 		captureCtx, cancelTimeout = context.WithTimeout(captureCtx, req.Duration)
144: 		defer cancelTimeout()
145: 	}
146: 
147: 	out, err := NewOutput(w, names, req.SnapLength, req.PacketLimit, l.window, stop)
148: 	if err != nil {
149: 		for _, source := range sources {
150: 			source.Close()
151: 		}
152: 
153: 		return err
154: 	}
155: 
156: 	var wg sync.WaitGroup
157: 	for idx, source := range sources {
158: 		reader := &localReader{
159: 			capture: l,
160: 			out:     out,
161: 			id:      uint32(idx), //nolint:gosec // bounded by the targets
162: 			target:  targets[idx],
163: 			snap:    req.SnapLength,
164: 			onGap:   req.OnGap,
165: 		}
166: 		wg.Go(func() {
167: 			reader.run(captureCtx, source)
168: 		})
169: 	}
170: 	wg.Wait()
171: 
172: 	return out.Close()
173: }
174: 
175: // openAll opens the sockets of every target before anything is written, one namespace entry
176: // per node, so a capture that cannot start fails without output.
177: func (l localCapture) openAll(
178: 	ctx context.Context,
179: 	targets []LocalTarget,
180: 	snapLength int,
181: ) ([]packetSource, error) {
182: 	type nodeTargets struct {
183: 		exec    ExecFunc
184: 		indexes []int
185: 		names   []string
186: 	}
187: 	var order []string
188: 	byNode := map[string]*nodeTargets{}
189: 	for idx, target := range targets {
190: 		group := byNode[target.Node]
191: 		if group == nil {
192: 			group = &nodeTargets{exec: target.Exec}
193: 			byNode[target.Node] = group
194: 			order = append(order, target.Node)
195: 		}
196: 		group.indexes = append(group.indexes, idx)
197: 		group.names = append(group.names, target.Interface)
198: 	}
199: 
200: 	sources := make([]packetSource, len(targets))
201: 	closeAll := func() {
202: 		for _, source := range sources {
203: 			if source != nil {
204: 				source.Close()
205: 			}
206: 		}
207: 	}
208: 	for _, node := range order {
209: 		group := byNode[node]
210: 		opened, err := l.open(ctx, group.exec, group.names, snapLength)
211: 		if err != nil {
212: 			closeAll()
213: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to start the capture on node %q: %w", node, err)
214: 		}
215: 		for idx, source := range opened {
216: 			sources[group.indexes[idx]] = source
217: 		}
218: 	}
219: 
220: 	return sources, nil
221: }
222: 
223: // localReader reads one interface into the merged stream.
224: type localReader struct {
225: 	capture localCapture
226: 	out     *Output
227: 	id      uint32
228: 	target  LocalTarget
229: 	snap    int
230: 	onGap   func(clablabruntime.CaptureGap)
231: }
232: 
233: func (r *localReader) run(ctx context.Context, source packetSource) {
234: 	defer func() {
235: 		if source != nil {
236: 			source.Close()
237: 		}
238: 	}()
239: 
240: 	for {
241: 		if ctx.Err() != nil {
242: 			r.drain(source, time.Now())
243: 			return
244: 		}
245: 
246: 		data, info, err := source.ReadPacket()
247: 		if errors.Is(err, errReadTimeout) {
248: 			continue
249: 		}
250: 		if err != nil {
251: 			// The socket reports an error once its interface went down or was removed, and
252: 			// keeps reporting it: it is reopened.
253: 			log.Debug("Capture socket failed, reopening it", "node", r.target.Node,
254: 				"interface", r.target.Interface, "error", err)
255: 			source = r.reopen(ctx, source)
256: 			if source == nil {
257: 				return
258: 			}
259: 
260: 			continue
261: 		}
262: 
263: 		if more, err := r.out.Emit(r.id, r.packet(data, info)); !more || err != nil {
264: 			return
265: 		}
266: 	}
267: }
268: 
269: // drain writes the packets that arrived before end but were still in the socket's ring: the
270: // kernel hands over a ring block once it is full or its block timeout passed, so the last
271: // packets before a capture ended may not have been read yet.
272: func (r *localReader) drain(source packetSource, end time.Time) {
273: 	deadline := end.Add(localDrainTimeout)
274: 	for time.Now().Before(deadline) {
275: 		data, info, err := source.ReadPacket()
276: 		if errors.Is(err, errReadTimeout) && time.Since(end) < localDrainQuiet {
277: 			continue
278: 		}
279: 		if err != nil || info.Timestamp.After(end) {
280: 			return
281: 		}
282: 		if more, err := r.out.Emit(r.id, r.packet(data, info)); !more || err != nil {
283: 			return
284: 		}
285: 	}
286: }
287: 
288: // packet copies a packet out of the socket's ring, cut to the snap length.
289: func (r *localReader) packet(data []byte, info gopacket.CaptureInfo) *Packet {
290: 	length := info.Length
291: 	for _, ancillary := range info.AncillaryData {
292: 		if _, ok := ancillary.(afpacket.AncillaryVLAN); ok {
293: 			// the tag was put back into the data and counts toward the frame length
294: 			length += vlanHeaderLength
295: 			break
296: 		}
297: 	}
298: 	length = max(length, len(data))
299: 
300: 	captured := make([]byte, min(len(data), r.snap))
301: 	copy(captured, data)
302: 
303: 	return &Packet{
304: 		Timestamp:      info.Timestamp.UnixNano(),
305: 		OriginalLength: uint32(length), //nolint:gosec // bounded by the interface MTU
306: 		Data:           captured,
307: 	}
308: }
309: 
310: // reopen opens the interface's socket again once its node and interface are back, and closes
311: // the failed one. It returns nil when the capture ended first. The time the interface was gone
312: // is reported as a gap: while it could not be opened, or when it left the failed socket's
313: // namespace, as a node restart that is over by the first attempt does. An interface that only
314: // went down is not, as it has no traffic while down.
315: func (r *localReader) reopen(ctx context.Context, failed packetSource) packetSource {
316: 	defer failed.Close()
317: 
318: 	from := time.Now()
319: 	var unavailable error
320: 	// gone is asked once the reopen ends: by then an interface that left the namespace was
321: 	// unregistered there, which the kernel does right after taking it down.
322: 	gone := func() error {
323: 		if unavailable != nil {
324: 			return unavailable
325: 		}
326: 		if failed.Removed() {
327: 			return errInterfaceRemoved
328: 		}
329: 
330: 		return nil
331: 	}
332: 
333: 	timer := time.NewTimer(r.capture.reopenInterval)
334: 	defer timer.Stop()
335: 
336: 	for {
337: 		select {
338: 		case <-ctx.Done():
339: 			if cause := gone(); cause != nil {
340: 				r.reportGap(from, time.Time{}, cause)
341: 			}
342: 
343: 			return nil
344: 		case <-timer.C:
345: 		}
346: 
347: 		opened, err := r.capture.open(ctx, r.target.Exec, []string{r.target.Interface}, r.snap)
348: 		if err != nil {
349: 			if unavailable == nil {
350: 				log.Debug("Capture interface is not available, waiting for it",
351: 					"node", r.target.Node, "interface", r.target.Interface, "error", err)
352: 			}
353: 			unavailable = err
354: 			resetTimer(timer, r.capture.reopenInterval)
355: 
356: 			continue
357: 		}
358: 		if cause := gone(); cause != nil {
359: 			r.reportGap(from, time.Now(), cause)
360: 		}
361: 
362: 		return opened[0]
363: 	}
364: }
365: 
366: func (r *localReader) reportGap(from, to time.Time, cause error) {
367: 	ReportGap(r.onGap, clablabruntime.CaptureGap{
368: 		Node:       r.target.Node,
369: 		Interfaces: []string{r.target.Interface},
370: 		From:       from,
371: 		To:         to,
372: 		Reason:     fmt.Sprintf("the interface was not available: %v", cause),
373: 	})
374: }
375: 
376: // openPacketSockets opens one packet socket per interface in the namespace exec enters. A socket
377: // stays bound to its interface after the thread left the namespace.
378: func openPacketSockets(
379: 	ctx context.Context,
380: 	exec ExecFunc,
381: 	interfaces []string,
382: 	snapLength int,
383: ) ([]packetSource, error) {
384: 	sources := make([]packetSource, 0, len(interfaces))
385: 	err := exec(ctx, func(ns.NetNS) error {
386: 		for _, name := range interfaces {
387: 			source, err := openPacketSocket(name, snapLength)
388: 			if err != nil {
389: 				return fmt.Errorf("interface %q: %w", name, err)
390: 			}
391: 			sources = append(sources, source)
392: 		}
393: 
394: 		return nil
395: 	})
396: 	if err != nil {
397: 		for _, source := range sources {
398: 			source.Close()
399: 		}
400: 
401: 		return nil, err
402: 	}
403: 
404: 	return sources, nil
405: }
406: 
407: // afpacketSource is a TPACKET_V3 receive ring on one interface, with a probe socket that tells
408: // whether the interface was removed.
409: type afpacketSource struct {
410: 	handle  *afpacket.TPacket
411: 	probe   int
412: 	ifindex int
413: }
414: 
415: func openPacketSocket(name string, snapLength int) (*afpacketSource, error) {
416: 	// A TPACKET_V3 block holds whole frames: it must be larger than the snap length, plus the
417: 	// block and frame headers, and a multiple of the page size.
418: 	pageSize := os.Getpagesize()
419: 	blockSize := max(localMinBlockSize, snapLength+pageSize)
420: 	blockSize = (blockSize + pageSize - 1) / pageSize * pageSize
421: 
422: 	handle, err := afpacket.NewTPacket(
423: 		afpacket.OptInterface(name),
424: 		afpacket.OptTPacketVersion(afpacket.TPacketVersion3),
425: 		afpacket.OptFrameSize(blockSize),
426: 		afpacket.OptBlockSize(blockSize),
427: 		afpacket.OptNumBlocks(localRingBlocks),
428: 		afpacket.OptBlockTimeout(localBlockTimeout),
429: 		afpacket.OptPollTimeout(localPollTimeout),
430: 		// the kernel moves 802.1Q tags out of the frame; captures show them in place
431: 		afpacket.OptAddVLANHeader(true),
432: 	)
433: 	if err != nil {
434: 		return nil, err
435: 	}
436: 	// promiscuous mode is a membership of this socket and ends with it
437: 	if err := handle.SetPromiscuous(true); err != nil {
438: 		handle.Close()
439: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to enable promiscuous mode: %w", err)
440: 	}
441: 	probe, ifindex, err := openRemovalProbe(name)
442: 	if err != nil {
443: 		handle.Close()
444: 		return nil, err
445: 	}
446: 
447: 	return &afpacketSource{handle: handle, probe: probe, ifindex: ifindex}, nil
448: }
449: 
450: // openRemovalProbe opens a packet socket bound to the interface that receives nothing: protocol
451: // 0 registers no packet handler. When the interface is unregistered from the namespace, deleted
452: // or moved to another one, the kernel resets the bound interface of the namespace's packet
453: // sockets to -1; when it only goes down, it keeps it. The afpacket handle does not expose its
454: // own socket, so the probe tells the two apart.
455: func openRemovalProbe(name string) (fd, ifindex int, err error) {
456: 	iface, err := net.InterfaceByName(name)
457: 	if err != nil {
458: 		return -1, 0, err
459: 	}
460: 	fd, err = unix.Socket(unix.AF_PACKET, unix.SOCK_RAW|unix.SOCK_CLOEXEC, 0)
461: 	if err != nil {
462: 		return -1, 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to open the interface probe: %w", err)
463: 	}
464: 	if err := unix.Bind(fd, &unix.SockaddrLinklayer{Ifindex: iface.Index}); err != nil {
465: 		unix.Close(fd)
466: 		return -1, 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to bind the interface probe: %w", err)
467: 	}
468: 
469: 	return fd, iface.Index, nil
470: }
471: 
472: func (s *afpacketSource) ReadPacket() ([]byte, gopacket.CaptureInfo, error) {
473: 	data, info, err := s.handle.ZeroCopyReadPacketData()
474: 	if errors.Is(err, afpacket.ErrTimeout) {
475: 		return nil, info, errReadTimeout
476: 	}
477: 
478: 	return data, info, err
479: }
480: 
481: func (s *afpacketSource) Removed() bool {
482: 	addr, err := unix.Getsockname(s.probe)
483: 	if err != nil {
484: 		return false
485: 	}
486: 	link, ok := addr.(*unix.SockaddrLinklayer)
487: 
488: 	return ok && link.Ifindex != s.ifindex
489: }
490: 
491: func (s *afpacketSource) Close() {
492: 	s.handle.Close()
493: 	unix.Close(s.probe)
494: }

(End of file - total 494 lines)

It\n\t// returns errReadTimeout when no packet arrived within the poll timeout.\n\tReadPacket() ([]byte, gopacket.CaptureInfo, error)\n\t// Removed reports whether the interface was removed from the source's network namespace,\n\t// deleted or moved to another one, as opposed to only going down.\n\tRemoved() bool\n\tClose()\n}\n\n// openSourcesFunc opens one packet source per interface in the network namespace exec enters.\ntype openSourcesFunc func(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\texec ExecFunc,\n\tinterfaces []string,\n\tsnapLength int,\n) ([]packetSource, error)\n\n// CaptureLocal streams pcapng from interfaces of nodes on this machine to w, with the same\n// output and end conditions as labruntime.PacketCapturer.Capture: one section, one Interface\n// Description Block per target named <node>:<interface>, then the packets of every target in\n// timestamp order. targets lists the interfaces of req.Targets, resolved, in the same order.\n//\n// Each interface is read through a packet socket opened in the node's network namespace, so\n// nothing runs in the node and the node image needs no capture tools; it needs CAP_NET_RAW and\n// the right to enter the namespace, so root. An interface that goes down is reopened once it is\n// up again; one that disappears (its node stopped or was recreated) is reattached when it is\n// back, and the time it was gone is reported as a gap through req.OnGap (or logged).\nfunc CaptureLocal(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\treq clablabruntime.CaptureRequest,\n\ttargets []LocalTarget,\n\tw io.Writer,\n) error {\n\treturn localCapture{\n\t\topen:           openPacketSockets,\n\t\twindow:         localReorderWindow,\n\t\treopenInterval: localReopenInterval,\n\t}.run(ctx, req, targets, w)\n}\n\n// localCapture is a local capture with its socket opener and timings, which tests replace.\ntype localCapture struct {\n\topen           openSourcesFunc\n\twindow         time.Duration\n\treopenInterval time.Duration\n}\n\nfunc (l localCapture) run(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\treq clablabruntime.CaptureRequest,\n\ttargets []LocalTarget,\n\tw io.Writer,\n) error {\n\tif err := ValidateRequest(&req); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tif len(targets) != len(req.Targets) {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"capture has %d resolved targets for %d requested ones\",\n\t\t\tlen(targets), len(req.Targets))\n\t}\n\n\tsources, err := l.openAll(ctx, targets, req.SnapLength)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tnames := make([]string, len(targets))\n\tfor idx, target := range targets {\n\t\tnames[idx] = target.Node + \":\" + target.Interface\n\t}\n\n\tcaptureCtx, stop := context.WithCancel(ctx)\n\tdefer stop()\n\tif req.Duration > 0 {\n\t\tvar cancelTimeout context.CancelFunc\n\t\tcaptureCtx, cancelTimeout = context.WithTimeout(captureCtx, req.Duration)\n\t\tdefer cancelTimeout()\n\t}\n\n\tout, err := NewOutput(w, names, req.SnapLength, req.PacketLimit, l.window, stop)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tfor _, source := range sources {\n\t\t\tsource.Close()\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tvar wg sync.WaitGroup\n\tfor idx, source := range sources {\n\t\treader := &localReader{\n\t\t\tcapture: l,\n\t\t\tout:     out,\n\t\t\tid:      uint32(idx), //nolint:gosec // bounded by the targets\n\t\t\ttarget:  targets[idx],\n\t\t\tsnap:    req.SnapLength,\n\t\t\tonGap:   req.OnGap,\n\t\t}\n\t\twg.Go(func() {\n\t\t\treader.run(captureCtx, source)\n\t\t})\n\t}\n\twg.Wait()\n\n\treturn out.Close()\n}\n\n// openAll opens the sockets of every target before anything is written, one namespace entry\n// per node, so a capture that cannot start fails without output.\nfunc (l localCapture) openAll(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\ttargets []LocalTarget,\n\tsnapLength int,\n) ([]packetSource, error) {\n\ttype nodeTargets struct {\n\t\texec    ExecFunc\n\t\tindexes []int\n\t\tnames   []string\n\t}\n\tvar order []string\n\tbyNode := map[string]*nodeTargets{}\n\tfor idx, target := range targets {\n\t\tgroup := byNode[target.Node]\n\t\tif group == nil {\n\t\t\tgroup = &nodeTargets{exec: target.Exec}\n\t\t\tbyNode[target.Node] = group\n\t\t\torder = append(order, target.Node)\n\t\t}\n\t\tgroup.indexes = append(group.indexes, idx)\n\t\tgroup.names = append(group.names, target.Interface)\n\t}\n\n\tsources := make([]packetSource, len(targets))\n\tcloseAll := func() {\n\t\tfor _, source := range sources {\n\t\t\tif source != nil {\n\t\t\t\tsource.Close()\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tfor _, node := range order {\n\t\tgroup := byNode[node]\n\t\topened, err := l.open(ctx, group.exec, group.names, snapLength)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tcloseAll()\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to start the capture on node %q: %w\", node, err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tfor idx, source := range opened {\n\t\t\tsources[group.indexes[idx]] = source\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn sources, nil\n}\n\n// localReader reads one interface into the merged stream.\ntype localReader struct {\n\tcapture localCapture\n\tout     *Output\n\tid      uint32\n\ttarget  LocalTarget\n\tsnap    int\n\tonGap   func(clablabruntime.CaptureGap)\n}\n\nfunc (r *localReader) run(ctx context.Context, source packetSource) {\n\tdefer func() {\n\t\tif source != nil {\n\t\t\tsource.Close()\n\t\t}\n\t}()\n\n\tfor {\n\t\tif ctx.Err() != nil {\n\t\t\tr.drain(source, time.Now())\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tdata, info, err := source.ReadPacket()\n\t\tif errors.Is(err, errReadTimeout) {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t// The socket reports an error once its interface went down or was removed, and\n\t\t\t// keeps reporting it: it is reopened.\n\t\t\tlog.Debug(\"Capture socket failed, reopening it\", \"node\", r.target.Node,\n\t\t\t\t\"interface\", r.target.Interface, \"error\", err)\n\t\t\tsource = r.reopen(ctx, source)\n\t\t\tif source == nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif more, err := r.out.Emit(r.id, r.packet(data, info)); !more || err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// drain writes the packets that arrived before end but were still in the socket's ring: the\n// kernel hands over a ring block once it is full or its block timeout passed, so the last\n// packets before a capture ended may not have been read yet.\nfunc (r *localReader) drain(source packetSource, end time.Time) {\n\tdeadline := end.Add(localDrainTimeout)\n\tfor time.Now().Before(deadline) {\n\t\tdata, info, err := source.ReadPacket()\n\t\tif errors.Is(err, errReadTimeout) && time.Since(end) < localDrainQuiet {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err != nil || info.Timestamp.After(end) {\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tif more, err := r.out.Emit(r.id, r.packet(data, info)); !more || err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// packet copies a packet out of the socket's ring, cut to the snap length.\nfunc (r *localReader) packet(data []byte, info gopacket.CaptureInfo) *Packet {\n\tlength := info.Length\n\tfor _, ancillary := range info.AncillaryData {\n\t\tif _, ok := ancillary.(afpacket.AncillaryVLAN); ok {\n\t\t\t// the tag was put back into the data and counts toward the frame length\n\t\t\tlength += vlanHeaderLength\n\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tlength = max(length, len(data))\n\n\tcaptured := make([]byte, min(len(data), r.snap))\n\tcopy(captured, data)\n\n\treturn &Packet{\n\t\tTimestamp:      info.Timestamp.UnixNano(),\n\t\tOriginalLength: uint32(length), //nolint:gosec // bounded by the interface MTU\n\t\tData:           captured,\n\t}\n}\n\n// reopen opens the interface's socket again once its node and interface are back, and closes\n// the failed one. It returns nil when the capture ended first. The time the interface was gone\n// is reported as a gap: while it could not be opened, or when it left the failed socket's\n// namespace, as a node restart that is over by the first attempt does. An interface that only\n// went down is not, as it has no traffic while down.\nfunc (r *localReader) reopen(ctx context.Context, failed packetSource) packetSource {\n\tdefer failed.Close()\n\n\tfrom := time.Now()\n\tvar unavailable error\n\t// gone is asked once the reopen ends: by then an interface that left the namespace was\n\t// unregistered there, which the kernel does right after taking it down.\n\tgone := func() error {\n\t\tif unavailable != nil {\n\t\t\treturn unavailable\n\t\t}\n\t\tif failed.Removed() {\n\t\t\treturn errInterfaceRemoved\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\ttimer := time.NewTimer(r.capture.reopenInterval)\n\tdefer timer.Stop()\n\n\tfor {\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n\t\t\tif cause := gone(); cause != nil {\n\t\t\t\tr.reportGap(from, time.Time{}, cause)\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\tcase <-timer.C:\n\t\t}\n\n\t\topened, err := r.capture.open(ctx, r.target.Exec, []string{r.target.Interface}, r.snap)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tif unavailable == nil {\n\t\t\t\tlog.Debug(\"Capture interface is not available, waiting for it\",\n\t\t\t\t\t\"node\", r.target.Node, \"interface\", r.target.Interface, \"error\", err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tunavailable = err\n\t\t\tresetTimer(timer, r.capture.reopenInterval)\n\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif cause := gone(); cause != nil {\n\t\t\tr.reportGap(from, time.Now(), cause)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn opened[0]\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (r *localReader) reportGap(from, to time.Time, cause error) {\n\tReportGap(r.onGap, clablabruntime.CaptureGap{\n\t\tNode:       r.target.Node,\n\t\tInterfaces: []string{r.target.Interface},\n\t\tFrom:       from,\n\t\tTo:         to,\n\t\tReason:     fmt.Sprintf(\"the interface was not available: %v\", cause),\n\t})\n}\n\n// openPacketSockets opens one packet socket per interface in the namespace exec enters. A socket\n// stays bound to its interface after the thread left the namespace.\nfunc openPacketSockets(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\texec ExecFunc,\n\tinterfaces []string,\n\tsnapLength int,\n) ([]packetSource, error) {\n\tsources := make([]packetSource, 0, len(interfaces))\n\terr := exec(ctx, func(ns.NetNS) error {\n\t\tfor _, name := range interfaces {\n\t\t\tsource, err := openPacketSocket(name, snapLength)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"interface %q: %w\", name, err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tsources = append(sources, source)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn nil\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tfor _, source := range sources {\n\t\t\tsource.Close()\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\treturn sources, nil\n}\n\n// afpacketSource is a TPACKET_V3 receive ring on one interface, with a probe socket that tells\n// whether the interface was removed.\ntype afpacketSource struct {\n\thandle  *afpacket.TPacket\n\tprobe   int\n\tifindex int\n}\n\nfunc openPacketSocket(name string, snapLength int) (*afpacketSource, error) {\n\t// A TPACKET_V3 block holds whole frames: it must be larger than the snap length, plus the\n\t// block and frame headers, and a multiple of the page size.\n\tpageSize := os.Getpagesize()\n\tblockSize := max(localMinBlockSize, snapLength+pageSize)\n\tblockSize = (blockSize + pageSize - 1) / pageSize * pageSize\n\n\thandle, err := afpacket.NewTPacket(\n\t\tafpacket.OptInterface(name),\n\t\tafpacket.OptTPacketVersion(afpacket.TPacketVersion3),\n\t\tafpacket.OptFrameSize(blockSize),\n\t\tafpacket.OptBlockSize(blockSize),\n\t\tafpacket.OptNumBlocks(localRingBlocks),\n\t\tafpacket.OptBlockTimeout(localBlockTimeout),\n\t\tafpacket.OptPollTimeout(localPollTimeout),\n\t\t// the kernel moves 802.1Q tags out of the frame; captures show them in place\n\t\tafpacket.OptAddVLANHeader(true),\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\t// promiscuous mode is a membership of this socket and ends with it\n\tif err := handle.SetPromiscuous(true); err != nil {\n\t\thandle.Close()\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to enable promiscuous mode: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tprobe, ifindex, err := openRemovalProbe(name)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\thandle.Close()\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\treturn &afpacketSource{handle: handle, probe: probe, ifindex: ifindex}, nil\n}\n\n// openRemovalProbe opens a packet socket bound to the interface that receives nothing: protocol\n// 0 registers no packet handler. When the interface is unregistered from the namespace, deleted\n// or moved to another one, the kernel resets the bound interface of the namespace's packet\n// sockets to -1; when it only goes down, it keeps it. The afpacket handle does not expose its\n// own socket, so the probe tells the two apart.\nfunc openRemovalProbe(name string) (fd, ifindex int, err error) {\n\tiface, err := net.InterfaceByName(name)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn -1, 0, err\n\t}\n\tfd, err = unix.Socket(unix.AF_PACKET, unix.SOCK_RAW|unix.SOCK_CLOEXEC, 0)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn -1, 0, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to open the interface probe: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tif err := unix.Bind(fd, &unix.SockaddrLinklayer{Ifindex: iface.Index}); err != nil {\n\t\tunix.Close(fd)\n\t\treturn -1, 0, fmt.Errorf(\"failed to bind the interface probe: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\treturn fd, iface.Index, nil\n}\n\nfunc (s *afpacketSource) ReadPacket() ([]byte, gopacket.CaptureInfo, error) {\n\tdata, info, err := s.handle.ZeroCopyReadPacketData()\n\tif errors.Is(err, afpacket.ErrTimeout) {\n\t\treturn nil, info, errReadTimeout\n\t}\n\n\treturn data, info, err\n}\n\nfunc (s *afpacketSource) Removed() bool {\n\taddr, err := unix.Getsockname(s.probe)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\tlink, ok := addr.(*unix.SockaddrLinklayer)\n\n\treturn ok && link.Ifindex != s.ifindex\n}\n\nfunc (s *afpacketSource) Close() {\n\ts.handle.Close()\n\tunix.Close(s.probe)\n}",

21: 	// write them in timestamp order. Each socket hands over its packets within
22: 	// localBlockTimeout, so a few times that covers the delivery skew between sockets.
23: 	localReorderWindow = 100 * time.Millisecond
24: 	// localBlockTimeout is how long the kernel fills a ring block before it hands it over.
25: 	localBlockTimeout = 10 * time.Millisecond
26: 	// localPollTimeout is how often a socket reader checks whether the capture ended.
27: 	localPollTimeout = 100 * time.Millisecond
28: 	// localReopenInterval is how often a reader tries to reopen the socket of an interface
29: 	// that went down or disappeared.
30: 	localReopenInterval = 200 * time.Millisecond
31: 	// localDrainQuiet is how long after the end of a capture a reader keeps waiting for packets
32: 	// still in the socket's ring: the kernel hands over a block within two block timeouts.
33: 	localDrainQuiet = 5 * localBlockTimeout
34: 	// localDrainTimeout bounds the drain while packets keep arriving.
35: 	localDrainTimeout = 250 * time.Millisecond
36: 	// localRingBlocks and localMinBlockSize size the receive ring of each socket: eight blocks

237: 
238: 		data, info, err := source.ReadPacket()
239: 		if errors.Is(err, errReadTimeout) {
240: 			continue
241: 		}
242: 		if err != nil {
243: 			// The socket reports an error once its interface went down or was removed, and
244: 			// keeps reporting it: it is reopened.
245: 			log.Debug("Capture socket failed, reopening it", "node", r.target.Node,
246: 				"interface", r.target.Interface, "error", err)
247: 			source.Close()
248: 			source = r.reopen(ctx)
249: 			if source == nil {
250: 				return
251: 			}
252: 
253: 			continue
254: 		}
255: 
256: 		if more, err := r.out.Emit(r.id, r.packet(data, info)); !more || err != nil {
257: 			return
258: 		}
259: 	}
260: }
261: 
262: // drain writes the packets that arrived before end but were still in the socket's ring: the
263: // kernel hands over a ring block once it is full or its block timeout passed, so the last
264: // packets before a capture ended may not have been read yet.
265: func (r *localReader) drain(source packetSource, end time.Time) {
266: 	deadline := end.Add(localDrainTimeout)
267: 	for time.Now().Before(deadline) {
268: 		data, info, err := source.ReadPacket()
269: 		if errors.Is(err, errReadTimeout) && time.Since(end) < localDrainQuiet {
270: 			continue
271: 		}
272: 		if err != nil || info.Timestamp.After(end) {
273: 			return
274: 		}
275: 		if more, err := r.out.Emit(r.id, r.packet(data, info)); !more || err != nil {
276: 			return
277: 		}
278: 	}
279: }
280: 
281: // packet copies a packet out of the socket's ring, cut to the snap length.
282: func (r *localReader) packet(data []byte, info gopacket.CaptureInfo) *Packet {
283: 	length := info.Length
284: 	for _, ancillary := range info.AncillaryData {
285: 		if _, ok := ancillary.(afpacket.AncillaryVLAN); ok {
286: 			// the tag was put back into the data and counts toward the frame length
287: 			length += vlanHeaderLength
288: 			break
289: 		}
290: 	}
291: 	length = max(length, len(data))
292: 
293: 	captured := make([]byte, min(len(data), r.snap))
294: 	copy(captured, data)
295: 
296: 	return &Packet{
297: 		Timestamp:      info.Timestamp.UnixNano(),
298: 		OriginalLength: uint32(length), //nolint:gosec // bounded by the interface MTU
299: 		Data:           captured,
300: 	}
301: }
302: 
303: // reopen opens the interface's socket again once its node and interface are back. It returns
304: // nil when the capture ended first. Time the interface could not be opened is reported as a gap;
305: // an interface that only went down is not, as it has no traffic while down.
306: func (r *localReader) reopen(ctx context.Context) packetSource {
307: 	from := time.Now()
308: 	var unavailable error
309: 
310: 	timer := time.NewTimer(r.capture.reopenInterval)
311: 	defer timer.Stop()
312: 
313: 	for {
314: 		select {
315: 		case <-ctx.Done():
316: 			if unavailable != nil {
317: 				r.reportGap(from, time.Time{}, unavailable)
318: 			}
319: 
320: 			return nil
321: 		case <-timer.C:
322: 		}
323: 
324: 		opened, err := r.capture.open(ctx, r.target.Exec, []string{r.target.Interface}, r.snap)
325: 		if err != nil {
326: 			if unavailable == nil {
327: 				log.Debug("Capture interface is not available, waiting for it",
328: 					"node", r.target.Node, "interface", r.target.Interface, "error", err)
329: 			}
330: 			unavailable = err
331: 			resetTimer(timer, r.capture.reopenInterval)
332: 
333: 			continue
334: 		}
335: 		if unavailable != nil {
336: 			r.reportGap(from, time.Now(), unavailable)
337: 		}
338: 
339: 		return opened[0]
340: 	}
341: }
342: 
343: func (r *localReader) reportGap(from, to time.Time, cause error) {
344: 	ReportGap(r.onGap, clablabruntime.CaptureGap{
345: 		Node:       r.target.Node,
346: 		Interfaces: []string{r.target.Interface},
347: 		From:       from,
348: 		To:         to,
349: 		Reason:     fmt.Sprintf("the interface was not available: %v", cause),
350: 	})
351: }
352: 
353: // openPacketSockets opens one packet socket per interface in the namespace exec enters. A socket
354: // stays bound to its interface after the thread left the namespace.
355: func openPacketSockets(
356: 	ctx context.Context,
357: 	exec ExecFunc,
358: 	interfaces []string,
359: 	snapLength int,
360: ) ([]packetSource, error) {
361: 	sources := make([]packetSource, 0, len(interfaces))
362: 	err := exec(ctx, func(ns.NetNS) error {
363: 		for _, name := range interfaces {
364: 			source, err := openPacketSocket(name, snapLength)
365: 			if err != nil {
366: 				return fmt.Errorf("interface %q: %w", name, err)
367: 			}
368: 			sources = append(sources, source)
369: 		}
370: 
371: 		return nil
372: 	})
373: 	if err != nil {
374: 		for _, source := range sources {
375: 			source.Close()
376: 		}
377: 
378: 		return nil, err
379: 	}
380: 
381: 	return sources, nil
382: }
383: 
384: // afpacketSource is a TPACKET_V3 receive ring on one interface.
385: type afpacketSource struct {
386: 	handle *afpacket.TPacket
387: }
388: 
389: func openPacketSocket(name string, snapLength int) (*afpacketSource, error) {
390: 	// A TPACKET_V3 block holds whole frames: it must be larger than the snap length, plus the
391: 	// block and frame headers, and a multiple of the page size.
392: 	pageSize := os.Getpagesize()
393: 	blockSize := max(localMinBlockSize, snapLength+pageSize)
394: 	blockSize = (blockSize + pageSize - 1) / pageSize * pageSize
395: 
396: 	handle, err := afpacket.NewTPacket(
397: 		afpacket.OptInterface(name),
398: 		afpacket.OptTPacketVersion(afpacket.TPacketVersion3),
399: 		afpacket.OptFrameSize(blockSize),
400: 		afpacket.OptBlockSize(blockSize),
401: 		afpacket.OptNumBlocks(localRingBlocks),
402: 		afpacket.OptBlockTimeout(localBlockTimeout),
403: 		afpacket.OptPollTimeout(localPollTimeout),
404: 		// the kernel moves 802.1Q tags out of the frame; captures show them in place
405: 		afpacket.OptAddVLANHeader(true),
406: 	)
407: 	if err != nil {
408: 		return nil, err
409: 	}
410: 	// promiscuous mode is a membership of this socket and ends with it
411: 	if err := handle.SetPromiscuous(true); err != nil {
412: 		handle.Close()
413: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to enable promiscuous mode: %w", err)
414: 	}
415: 
416: 	return &afpacketSource{handle: handle}, nil
417: }
418: 
419: func (s *afpacketSource) ReadPacket() ([]byte, gopacket.CaptureInfo, error) {
420: 	data, info, err := s.handle.ZeroCopyReadPacketData()
421: 	if errors.Is(err, afpacket.ErrTimeout) {
422: 		return nil, info, errReadTimeout
423: 	}
424: 
425: 	return data, info, err
426: }
427: 
428: func (s *afpacketSource) Close() {
429: 	s.handle.Close()
430: }

37: 	closed  chan struct{}
38: 	once    sync.Once
39: 	pending *fakeFrame
40: 	// removed is what Removed reports: the interface left the source's namespace.
41: 	removed atomic.Bool
42: }
43: 
44: func newFakeSource() *fakeSource {
45: 	return &fakeSource{frames: make(chan fakeFrame, 64), closed: make(chan struct{})}
46: }
47: 
48: func (s *fakeSource) ReadPacket() ([]byte, gopacket.CaptureInfo, error) {
49: 	if s.pending == nil {
50: 		select {
51: 		case frame := <-s.frames:
52: 			s.pending = &frame
53: 		case <-time.After(5 * time.Millisecond):
54: 			return nil, gopacket.CaptureInfo{}, errReadTimeout
55: 		}
56: 	}
57: 	if wait := time.Until(s.pending.visible); wait > 0 {
58: 		time.Sleep(min(wait, 5*time.Millisecond))
59: 		if time.Now().Before(s.pending.visible) {
60: 			return nil, gopacket.CaptureInfo{}, errReadTimeout
61: 		}
62: 	}
63: 
64: 	{
65: 		frame := *s.pending
66: 		s.pending = nil
67: 		if frame.err != nil {
68: 			return nil, gopacket.CaptureInfo{}, frame.err
69: 		}
70: 		info := gopacket.CaptureInfo{
71: 			Timestamp: frame.stamp, CaptureLength: len(frame.data), Length: frame.length,
72: 		}
73: 		if frame.vlan {
74: 			info.AncillaryData = []any{afpacket.AncillaryVLAN{VLAN: 10}}
75: 		}
76: 
77: 		return frame.data, info, nil
78: 	}
79: }
80: 
81: func (s *fakeSource) Removed() bool {
82: 	return s.removed.Load()
83: }
84: 
85: func (s *fakeSource) Close() {
86: 	s.once.Do(func() { close(s.closed) })
87: }
88: 
89: // fakeHost hands out fake sources per node and interface. Opening an interface listed in
90: // missing fails, like an interface whose node does not run.
91: type fakeHost struct {
92: 	mu      sync.Mutex
93: 	opened  map[string][]*fakeSource
94: 	missing map[string]bool
95: 	ready   chan string
96: }
97: 
98: func newFakeHost() *fakeHost {
99: 	return &fakeHost{
100: 		opened: map[string][]*fakeSource{}, missing: map[string]bool{},
101: 		ready: make(chan string, 64),
102: 	}
103: }
104: 
105: func (h *fakeHost) exec(node string) ExecFunc {
106: 	return func(_ context.Context, f func(ns.NetNS) error) error {
107: 		h.mu.Lock()
108: 		missing := h.missing[node]
109: 		h.mu.Unlock()
110: 		if missing {
111: 			return fmt.Errorf("node %q: container network namespace is not available", node)
112: 		}
113: 
114: 		return f(nil)
115: 	}
116: }
117: 
118: func (h *fakeHost) open(
119: 	ctx context.Context,
120: 	exec ExecFunc,
121: 	interfaces []string,
122: 	_ int,
123: ) ([]packetSource, error) {
124: 	var node string
125: 	err := exec(ctx, func(ns.NetNS) error { return nil })
126: 	if err != nil {
127: 		return nil, err
128: 	}
129: 
130: 	h.mu.Lock()
131: 	defer h.mu.Unlock()
132: 	sources := make([]packetSource, 0, len(interfaces))
133: 	for _, name := range interfaces {
134: 		if h.missing[name] {
135: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("interface %q: no such network interface", name)
136: 		}
137: 		node = name
138: 		source := newFakeSource()
139: 		h.opened[name] = append(h.opened[name], source)
140: 		sources = append(sources, source)
141: 		h.ready <- node
142: 	}
143: 
144: 	return sources, nil
145: }
146: 
147: func (h *fakeHost) source(name string, generation int) *fakeSource {
148: 	h.mu.Lock()
149: 	defer h.mu.Unlock()
150: 
151: 	return h.opened[name][generation]
152: }
153: 
154: func (h *fakeHost) setMissing(name string, missing bool) {
155: 	h.mu.Lock()
156: 	defer h.mu.Unlock()
157: 	h.missing[name] = missing
158: }
159: 
160: func (h *fakeHost) capture() localCapture {
161: 	return localCapture{
162: 		open:           h.open,
163: 		window:         20 * time.Millisecond,
164: 		reopenInterval: time.Millisecond,
165: 	}
166: }
167: 
168: // waitOpened waits until n sources were opened.
169: func (h *fakeHost) waitOpened(t *testing.T, n int) {
170: 	t.Helper()
171: 	for range n {
172: 		select {
173: 		case <-h.ready:
174: 		case <-time.After(2 * time.Second):
175: 			t.Fatal("source was not opened")
176: 		}
177: 	}
178: }
179: 
180: type parsedLocalCapture struct {
181: 	interfaces []string
182: 	packets    []Packet
183: }
184: 
185: func parseLocalCapture(t *testing.T, data []byte) parsedLocalCapture {
186: 	t.Helper()
187: 
188: 	var parsed parsedLocalCapture
189: 	reader := NewReader(bytes.NewReader(data))
190: 	for {
191: 		event, err := reader.Next()
192: 		if errors.Is(err, io.EOF) {
193: 			break
194: 		}
195: 		if err != nil {
196: 			t.Fatalf("invalid capture: %v", err)
197: 		}
198: 		if event.Interface != nil {
199: 			parsed.interfaces = append(parsed.interfaces, event.Interface.Name)
200: 		}
201: 		if event.Packet != nil {
202: 			parsed.packets = append(parsed.packets, *event.Packet)
203: 		}
204: 	}
205: 	if reader.Sections() != 1 {
206: 		t.Fatalf("capture has %d sections", reader.Sections())
207: 	}
208: 
209: 	return parsed
210: }
211: 
212: func localRequest(packetLimit int, duration time.Duration) clablabruntime.CaptureRequest {
213: 	return clablabruntime.CaptureRequest{
214: 		Name: "lab",
215: 		Targets: []clablabruntime.CaptureTarget{
216: 			{Node: "r1", Interface: "eth1"}, {Node: "r2", Interface: "eth1"},
217: 		},
218: 		PacketLimit: packetLimit,
219: 		Duration:    duration,
220: 		SnapLength:  64,
221: 	}
222: }
223: 
224: func TestCaptureLocalMergesInterfacesInTimestampOrder(t *testing.T) {
225: 	t.Parallel()
226: 
227: 	host := newFakeHost()
228: 	// the sources are keyed by interface name in the fake host, so the two nodes use distinct
229: 	// names behind the requested ones
230: 	targets := []LocalTarget{
231: 		{Node: "r1", Interface: "e1", Exec: host.exec("r1")},
232: 		{Node: "r2", Interface: "e2", Exec: host.exec("r2")},
233: 	}
234: 
235: 	var out bytes.Buffer
236: 	done := make(chan error, 1)
237: 	go func() {
238: 		done <- host.capture().run(context.Background(), localRequest(4, 0), targets, &out)
239: 	}()
240: 	host.waitOpened(t, 2)
241: 
242: 	base := time.Unix(1_761_559_200, 0)
243: 	// r2's packets reach containerlab first although r1 stamped one of them earlier
244: 	host.source("e2", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{
245: 		data: []byte("r2-a"), stamp: base.Add(2 * time.Millisecond), length: 4,
246: 	}
247: 	host.source("e2", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{
248: 		data: bytes.Repeat([]byte{7}, 100), stamp: base.Add(4 * time.Millisecond), length: 100,
249: 	}
250: 	host.source("e1", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{
251: 		data: []byte("r1-a"), stamp: base.Add(1 * time.Millisecond), length: 4,
252: 	}
253: 	host.source("e1", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{
254: 		data: []byte("r1-v"), stamp: base.Add(3 * time.Millisecond), length: 60, vlan: true,
255: 	}
256: 
257: 	select {
258: 	case err := <-done:
259: 		if err != nil {
260: 			t.Fatal(err)
261: 		}
262: 	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
263: 		t.Fatal("capture did not stop at the packet limit")
264: 	}
265: 
266: 	parsed := parseLocalCapture(t, out.Bytes())
267: 	if want := []string{"r1:e1", "r2:e2"}; !slices.Equal(parsed.interfaces, want) {
268: 		t.Fatalf("interfaces = %v, want %v", parsed.interfaces, want)
269: 	}
270: 	if len(parsed.packets) != 4 {
271: 		t.Fatalf("captured %d packets, want 4", len(parsed.packets))
272: 	}
273: 	var ids []uint32
274: 	for idx, packet := range parsed.packets {
275: 		ids = append(ids, packet.InterfaceID)
276: 		if want := base.Add(time.Duration(idx+1) * time.Millisecond).
277: 			UnixNano(); packet.Timestamp != want {
278: 			t.Fatalf("packet %d stamped %d, want %d", idx, packet.Timestamp, want)
279: 		}
280: 	}
281: 	if !slices.Equal(ids, []uint32{0, 1, 0, 1}) {
282: 		t.Fatalf("interface IDs = %v", ids)
283: 	}
284: 	// a re-inserted VLAN tag counts toward the frame length
285: 	if parsed.packets[2].OriginalLength != 64 {
286: 		t.Fatalf("VLAN frame length = %d, want 64", parsed.packets[2].OriginalLength)
287: 	}
288: 	// frames are cut to the snap length
289: 	if last := parsed.packets[3]; len(last.Data) != 64 || last.OriginalLength != 100 {
290: 		t.Fatalf("long frame kept %d of %d bytes", len(last.Data), last.OriginalLength)
291: 	}
292: 	for _, name := range []string{"e1", "e2"} {
293: 		select {
294: 		case <-host.source(name, 0).closed:
295: 		default:
296: 			t.Fatalf("source %s was not closed", name)
297: 		}
298: 	}
299: }

301: func TestCaptureLocalEndsAfterTheDurationOrWhenCanceled(t *testing.T) {
302: 	t.Parallel()
303: 
304: 	host := newFakeHost()
305: 	targets := []LocalTarget{
306: 		{Node: "r1", Interface: "e1", Exec: host.exec("r1")},
307: 		{Node: "r1", Interface: "e2", Exec: host.exec("r1")},
308: 	}
309: 
310: 	var out bytes.Buffer
311: 	start := time.Now()
312: 	if err := host.capture().run(context.Background(), localRequest(0, 50*time.Millisecond),
313: 		targets, &out); err != nil {
314: 		t.Fatal(err)
315: 	}
316: 	if elapsed := time.Since(start); elapsed < 50*time.Millisecond || elapsed > 2*time.Second {
317: 		t.Fatalf("capture ran %s for a 50ms duration", elapsed)
318: 	}

320: 		t.Fatalf("interfaces = %v", parsed.interfaces)
321: 	}

325: 	go func() {
326: 		done <- host.capture().run(ctx, localRequest(0, 0), targets, io.Discard)
327: 	}()
328: 	host.waitOpened(t, 4)
329: 	cancel()

333: 			t.Fatalf("canceled capture returned %v", err)
334: 		}
335: 	case <-time.After(2 * time.Second):
336: 		t.Fatal("canceled capture did not end")
337: 	}
338: }

402: func TestCaptureLocalReopensInterfacesAndReportsWhileTheyWereGone(t *testing.T) {
403: 	t.Parallel()
404: 
405: 	host := newFakeHost()
406: 	targets := []LocalTarget{{Node: "r1", Interface: "e1", Exec: host.exec("r1")}}
407: 	req := localRequest(2, 0)
408: 	req.Targets = req.Targets[:1]
409: 
410: 	var mu sync.Mutex
411: 	var gaps []clablabruntime.CaptureGap
412: 	req.OnGap = func(gap clablabruntime.CaptureGap) {
413: 		mu.Lock()
414: 		defer mu.Unlock()
415: 		gaps = append(gaps, gap)
416: 	}
417: 
418: 	var out bytes.Buffer
419: 	done := make(chan error, 1)
420: 	go func() {
421: 		done <- host.capture().run(context.Background(), req, targets, &out)
422: 	}()
423: 	host.waitOpened(t, 1)
424: 
425: 	base := time.Unix(1_761_559_200, 0)
426: 	host.source("e1", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{data: []byte("a"), stamp: base, length: 1}
427: 
428: 	// the interface goes down: the socket fails and is reopened at once, without a gap
429: 	host.source("e1", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{err: afpacket.ErrPoll}
430: 	host.waitOpened(t, 1)
431: 
432: 	// the node restarts: the interface is gone for a while, then back
433: 	host.setMissing("r1", true)
434: 	host.source("e1", 1).frames <- fakeFrame{err: afpacket.ErrPoll}
435: 	time.Sleep(20 * time.Millisecond)
436: 	host.setMissing("r1", false)
437: 	host.waitOpened(t, 1)
438: 	host.source("e1", 2).frames <- fakeFrame{
439: 		data: []byte("b"), stamp: base.Add(time.Second), length: 1,
440: 	}
441: 
442: 	select {
443: 	case err := <-done:
444: 		if err != nil {
445: 			t.Fatal(err)
446: 		}
447: 	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
448: 		t.Fatal("capture did not stop at the packet limit")
449: 	}
450: 
451: 	if parsed := parseLocalCapture(t, out.Bytes()); len(parsed.packets) != 2 {
452: 		t.Fatalf("captured %d packets across the reopen, want 2", len(parsed.packets))
453: 	}
454: 	mu.Lock()
455: 	defer mu.Unlock()
456: 	if len(gaps) != 1 {
457: 		t.Fatalf("gaps = %+v, want one for the restart", gaps)
458: 	}
459: 	gap := gaps[0]
460: 	if gap.Node != "r1" || !slices.Equal(gap.Interfaces, []string{"e1"}) || gap.To.IsZero() ||
461: 		!gap.To.After(gap.From) || !strings.Contains(gap.Reason, "not available") {
462: 		t.Fatalf("gap = %+v", gap)
463: 	}
464: }

517: 		t.Fatalf("gap = %+v", gap)
518: 	}
519: }

45: func (s *fakeSource) ReadPacket() ([]byte, gopacket.CaptureInfo, error) {
46: 	if s.pending == nil {
47: 		select {
48: 		case frame := <-s.frames:
49: 			s.pending = &frame
50: 		case <-time.After(5 * time.Millisecond):
51: 			return nil, gopacket.CaptureInfo{}, errReadTimeout
52: 		}
53: 	}
54: 	if wait := time.Until(s.pending.visible); wait > 0 {
55: 		time.Sleep(min(wait, 5*time.Millisecond))
56: 		if time.Now().Before(s.pending.visible) {
57: 			return nil, gopacket.CaptureInfo{}, errReadTimeout
58: 		}
59: 	}
60: 
61: 	{
62: 		frame := *s.pending
63: 		s.pending = nil
64: 		if frame.err != nil {
65: 			return nil, gopacket.CaptureInfo{}, frame.err
66: 		}
67: 		info := gopacket.CaptureInfo{
68: 			Timestamp: frame.stamp, CaptureLength: len(frame.data), Length: frame.length,
69: 		}
70: 		if frame.vlan {
71: 			info.AncillaryData = []any{afpacket.AncillaryVLAN{VLAN: 10}}
72: 		}
73: 
74: 		return frame.data, info, nil
75: 	}

95: 	}

98: func (h *fakeHost) exec(node string) ExecFunc {
99: 	return func(_ context.Context, f func(ns.NetNS) error) error {

102: 		h.mu.Unlock()

104: 			return fmt.Errorf("node %q: container network namespace is not available", node)
105: 		}

124: 	defer h.mu.Unlock()
125: 	sources := make([]packetSource, 0, len(interfaces))
126: 	for _, name := range interfaces {
127: 		if h.missing[name] {
128: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("interface %q: no such network interface", name)
129: 		}
130: 		node = name
131: 		source := newFakeSource()
132: 		h.opened[name] = append(h.opened[name], source)
133: 		sources = append(sources, source)
134: 		h.ready <- node
135: 	}
136: 
137: 	return sources, nil
138: }
139: 
140: func (h *fakeHost) source(name string, generation int) *fakeSource {
141: 	h.mu.Lock()
142: 	defer h.mu.Unlock()
143: 
144: 	return h.opened[name][generation]
145: }

148: 	h.mu.Lock()
149: 	defer h.mu.Unlock()
150: 	h.missing[name] = missing
151: }

161: // waitOpened waits until n sources were opened.
162: func (h *fakeHost) waitOpened(t *testing.T, n int) {
163: 	t.Helper()
164: 	for range n {
165: 		select {
166: 		case <-h.ready:
167: 		case <-time.After(2 * time.Second):
168: 			t.Fatal("source was not opened")
169: 		}
170: 	}

173: type parsedLocalCapture struct {
174: 	interfaces []string
175: 	packets    []Packet
176: }
177: 
178: func parseLocalCapture(t *testing.T, data []byte) parsedLocalCapture {
179: 	t.Helper()
180: 
181: 	var parsed parsedLocalCapture
182: 	reader := NewReader(bytes.NewReader(data))
183: 	for {
184: 		event, err := reader.Next()
185: 		if errors.Is(err, io.EOF) {
186: 			break
187: 		}
188: 		if err != nil {
189: 			t.Fatalf("invalid capture: %v", err)
190: 		}
191: 		if event.Interface != nil {
192: 			parsed.interfaces = append(parsed.interfaces, event.Interface.Name)
193: 		}
194: 		if event.Packet != nil {
195: 			parsed.packets = append(parsed.packets, *event.Packet)
196: 		}
197: 	}
198: 	if reader.Sections() != 1 {
199: 		t.Fatalf("capture has %d sections", reader.Sections())
200: 	}
201: 
202: 	return parsed
203: }

205: func localRequest(packetLimit int, duration time.Duration) clablabruntime.CaptureRequest {
206: 	return clablabruntime.CaptureRequest{
207: 		Name: "lab",
208: 		Targets: []clablabruntime.CaptureTarget{
209: 			{Node: "r1", Interface: "eth1"}, {Node: "r2", Interface: "eth1"},
210: 		},
211: 		PacketLimit: packetLimit,
212: 		Duration:    duration,
213: 		SnapLength:  64,
214: 	}
215: }

217: func TestCaptureLocalMergesInterfacesInTimestampOrder(t *testing.T) {
218: 	t.Parallel()
219: 
220: 	host := newFakeHost()
221: 	// the sources are keyed by interface name in the fake host, so the two nodes use distinct
222: 	// names behind the requested ones
223: 	targets := []LocalTarget{
224: 		{Node: "r1", Interface: "e1", Exec: host.exec("r1")},
225: 		{Node: "r2", Interface: "e2", Exec: host.exec("r2")},
226: 	}
227: 
228: 	var out bytes.Buffer
229: 	done := make(chan error, 1)
230: 	go func() {
231: 		done <- host.capture().run(context.Background(), localRequest(4, 0), targets, &out)
232: 	}()
233: 	host.waitOpened(t, 2)
234: 
235: 	base := time.Unix(1_761_559_200, 0)
236: 	// r2's packets reach containerlab first although r1 stamped one of them earlier
237: 	host.source("e2", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{
238: 		data: []byte("r2-a"), stamp: base.Add(2 * time.Millisecond), length: 4,
239: 	}
240: 	host.source("e2", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{
241: 		data: bytes.Repeat([]byte{7}, 100), stamp: base.Add(4 * time.Millisecond), length: 100,
242: 	}
243: 	host.source("e1", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{
244: 		data: []byte("r1-a"), stamp: base.Add(1 * time.Millisecond), length: 4,
245: 	}
246: 	host.source("e1", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{
247: 		data: []byte("r1-v"), stamp: base.Add(3 * time.Millisecond), length: 60, vlan: true,
248: 	}
249: 
250: 	select {
251: 	case err := <-done:
252: 		if err != nil {
253: 			t.Fatal(err)
254: 		}
255: 	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
256: 		t.Fatal("capture did not stop at the packet limit")
257: 	}
258: 
259: 	parsed := parseLocalCapture(t, out.Bytes())
260: 	if want := []string{"r1:e1", "r2:e2"}; !slices.Equal(parsed.interfaces, want) {
261: 		t.Fatalf("interfaces = %v, want %v", parsed.interfaces, want)
262: 	}
263: 	if len(parsed.packets) != 4 {
264: 		t.Fatalf("captured %d packets, want 4", len(parsed.packets))
265: 	}
266: 	var ids []uint32
267: 	for idx, packet := range parsed.packets {
268: 		ids = append(ids, packet.InterfaceID)
269: 		if want := base.Add(time.Duration(idx+1) * time.Millisecond).
270: 			UnixNano(); packet.Timestamp != want {
271: 			t.Fatalf("packet %d stamped %d, want %d", idx, packet.Timestamp, want)
272: 		}
273: 	}
274: 	if !slices.Equal(ids, []uint32{0, 1, 0, 1}) {
275: 		t.Fatalf("interface IDs = %v", ids)
276: 	}
277: 	// a re-inserted VLAN tag counts toward the frame length
278: 	if parsed.packets[2].OriginalLength != 64 {
279: 		t.Fatalf("VLAN frame length = %d, want 64", parsed.packets[2].OriginalLength)
280: 	}
281: 	// frames are cut to the snap length
282: 	if last := parsed.packets[3]; len(last.Data) != 64 || last.OriginalLength != 100 {
283: 		t.Fatalf("long frame kept %d of %d bytes", len(last.Data), last.OriginalLength)
284: 	}
285: 	for _, name := range []string{"e1", "e2"} {
286: 		select {
287: 		case <-host.source(name, 0).closed:
288: 		default:
289: 			t.Fatalf("source %s was not closed", name)
290: 		}
291: 	}
292: }
293: 
294: func TestCaptureLocalEndsAfterTheDurationOrWhenCanceled(t *testing.T) {
295: 	t.Parallel()
296: 
297: 	host := newFakeHost()
298: 	targets := []LocalTarget{
299: 		{Node: "r1", Interface: "e1", Exec: host.exec("r1")},
300: 		{Node: "r1", Interface: "e2", Exec: host.exec("r1")},
301: 	}
302: 
303: 	var out bytes.Buffer
304: 	start := time.Now()
305: 	if err := host.capture().run(context.Background(), localRequest(0, 50*time.Millisecond),
306: 		targets, &out); err != nil {
307: 		t.Fatal(err)
308: 	}
309: 	if elapsed := time.Since(start); elapsed < 50*time.Millisecond || elapsed > 2*time.Second {
310: 		t.Fatalf("capture ran %s for a 50ms duration", elapsed)
311: 	}
312: 	if parsed := parseLocalCapture(t, out.Bytes()); len(parsed.interfaces) != 2 {
313: 		t.Fatalf("interfaces = %v", parsed.interfaces)
314: 	}
315: 
316: 	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
317: 	done := make(chan error, 1)
318: 	go func() {
319: 		done <- host.capture().run(ctx, localRequest(0, 0), targets, io.Discard)
320: 	}()
321: 	host.waitOpened(t, 4)
322: 	cancel()
323: 	select {
324: 	case err := <-done:
325: 		if err != nil {
326: 			t.Fatalf("canceled capture returned %v", err)
327: 		}
328: 	case <-time.After(2 * time.Second):
329: 		t.Fatal("canceled capture did not end")
330: 	}
331: }

333: func TestCaptureLocalWritesPacketsStillInTheRingWhenItEnds(t *testing.T) {
334: 	t.Parallel()
335: 
336: 	host := newFakeHost()
337: 	targets := []LocalTarget{{Node: "r1", Interface: "e1", Exec: host.exec("r1")}}
338: 	req := localRequest(0, 0)
339: 	req.Targets = req.Targets[:1]
340: 
341: 	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
342: 	var out bytes.Buffer
343: 	done := make(chan error, 1)
344: 	go func() {
345: 		done <- host.capture().run(ctx, req, targets, &out)
346: 	}()
347: 	host.waitOpened(t, 1)
348: 
349: 	now := time.Now()
350: 	// stamped before the end, handed over by the kernel only after it
351: 	host.source("e1", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{
352: 		data: []byte("before"), stamp: now, length: 6, visible: now.Add(30 * time.Millisecond),
353: 	}
354: 	// stamped after the end: not part of the capture
355: 	host.source("e1", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{
356: 		data: []byte("after"), stamp: now.Add(time.Hour), length: 5,
357: 		visible: now.Add(40 * time.Millisecond),
358: 	}
359: 	cancel()
360: 
361: 	if err := <-done; err != nil {
362: 		t.Fatal(err)
363: 	}
364: 	parsed := parseLocalCapture(t, out.Bytes())
365: 	if len(parsed.packets) != 1 || string(parsed.packets[0].Data) != "before" {
366: 		t.Fatalf("packets = %+v, want only the one stamped before the end", parsed.packets)
367: 	}
368: }

395: func TestCaptureLocalReopensInterfacesAndReportsWhileTheyWereGone(t *testing.T) {
396: 	t.Parallel()
397: 
398: 	host := newFakeHost()
399: 	targets := []LocalTarget{{Node: "r1", Interface: "e1", Exec: host.exec("r1")}}
400: 	req := localRequest(2, 0)
401: 	req.Targets = req.Targets[:1]
402: 
403: 	var mu sync.Mutex
404: 	var gaps []clablabruntime.CaptureGap
405: 	req.OnGap = func(gap clablabruntime.CaptureGap) {
406: 		mu.Lock()
407: 		defer mu.Unlock()
408: 		gaps = append(gaps, gap)
409: 	}
410: 
411: 	var out bytes.Buffer
412: 	done := make(chan error, 1)
413: 	go func() {
414: 		done <- host.capture().run(context.Background(), req, targets, &out)
415: 	}()
416: 	host.waitOpened(t, 1)
417: 
418: 	base := time.Unix(1_761_559_200, 0)
419: 	host.source("e1", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{data: []byte("a"), stamp: base, length: 1}
420: 
421: 	// the interface goes down: the socket fails and is reopened at once, without a gap
422: 	host.source("e1", 0).frames <- fakeFrame{err: afpacket.ErrPoll}
423: 	host.waitOpened(t, 1)
424: 
425: 	// the node restarts: the interface is gone for a while, then back
426: 	host.setMissing("r1", true)
427: 	host.source("e1", 1).frames <- fakeFrame{err: afpacket.ErrPoll}
428: 	time.Sleep(20 * time.Millisecond)
429: 	host.setMissing("r1", false)
430: 	host.waitOpened(t, 1)
431: 	host.source("e1", 2).frames <- fakeFrame{
432: 		data: []byte("b"), stamp: base.Add(time.Second), length: 1,
433: 	}
434: 
435: 	select {
436: 	case err := <-done:
437: 		if err != nil {
438: 			t.Fatal(err)
439: 		}
440: 	case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
441: 		t.Fatal("capture did not stop at the packet limit")
442: 	}
443: 
444: 	if parsed := parseLocalCapture(t, out.Bytes()); len(parsed.packets) != 2 {
445: 		t.Fatalf("captured %d packets across the reopen, want 2", len(parsed.packets))
446: 	}
447: 	mu.Lock()
448: 	defer mu.Unlock()
449: 	if len(gaps) != 1 {
450: 		t.Fatalf("gaps = %+v, want one for the restart", gaps)
451: 	}
452: 	gap := gaps[0]
453: 	if gap.Node != "r1" || !slices.Equal(gap.Interfaces, []string{"e1"}) || gap.To.IsZero() ||
454: 		!gap.To.After(gap.From) || !strings.Contains(gap.Reason, "not available") {
455: 		t.Fatalf("gap = %+v", gap)
456: 	}
457: }

wait > 0 {\n\t\ttime.Sleep(min(wait, 5*time.Millisecond))\n\t\tif time.Now().Before(s.pending.visible) {\n\t\t\treturn nil, gopacket.CaptureInfo{}, errReadTimeout\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t{\n\t\tframe := *s.pending\n\t\ts.pending = nil\n\t\tif frame.err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, gopacket.CaptureInfo{}, frame.err\n\t\t}\n\t\tinfo := gopacket.CaptureInfo{\n\t\t\tTimestamp: frame.stamp, CaptureLength: len(frame.data), Length: frame.length,\n\t\t}\n\t\tif frame.vlan {\n\t\t\tinfo.AncillaryData = []any{afpacket.AncillaryVLAN{VLAN: 10}}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn frame.data, info, nil\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (s *fakeSource) Close() {\n\ts.once.Do(func() { close(s.closed) })\n}\n\n// fakeHost hands out fake sources per node and interface. Opening an interface listed in\n// missing fails, like an interface whose node does not run.\ntype fakeHost struct {\n\tmu      sync.Mutex\n\topened  map[string][]*fakeSource\n\tmissing map[string]bool\n\tready   chan string\n}\n\nfunc newFakeHost() *fakeHost {\n\treturn &fakeHost{\n\t\topened: map[string][]*fakeSource{}, missing: map[string]bool{},\n\t\tready: make(chan string, 64),\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (h *fakeHost) exec(node string) ExecFunc {\n\treturn func(_ context.Context, f func(ns.NetNS) error) error {\n\t\th.mu.Lock()\n\t\tmissing := h.missing[node]\n\t\th.mu.Unlock()\n\t\tif missing {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node %q: container network namespace is not available\", node)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn f(nil)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (h *fakeHost) open(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\texec ExecFunc,\n\tinterfaces []string,\n\t_ int,\n) ([]packetSource, error) {\n\tvar node string\n\terr := exec(ctx, func(ns.NetNS) error { return nil })\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\th.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer h.mu.Unlock()\n\tsources := make([]packetSource, 0, len(interfaces))\n\tfor _, name := range interfaces {\n\t\tif h.missing[name] {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"interface %q: no such network interface\", name)\n\t\t}\n\t\tnode = name\n\t\tsource := newFakeSource()\n\t\th.opened[name] = append(h.opened[name], source)\n\t\tsources = append(sources, source)\n\t\th.ready <- node\n\t}\n\n\treturn sources, nil\n}\n\nfunc (h *fakeHost) source(name string, generation int) *fakeSource {\n\th.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer h.mu.Unlock()\n\n\treturn h.opened[name][generation]\n}\n\nfunc (h *fakeHost) setMissing(name string, missing bool) {\n\th.mu.Lock()\n\tdefer h.mu.Unlock()\n\th.missing[name] = missing\n}\n\nfunc (h waits until n sources were opened.\nfunc (h *fakeHost) waitOpened(t *testing.T, n int) {\n\tt.Helper()\n\tfor range n {\n\t\tselect {\n\t\tcase <-h.ready:\n\t\tcase <-time.After(2 * time.Second):\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"source was not opened\")\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\ntype parsedLocalCapture struct {\n\tinterfaces []string\n\tpackets    []Packet\n}\n\nfunc parseLocalCapture(t *testing.T, data Reasoning
Now let me analyze the change carefully.

**Change summary:**
- `packetSource` gains `Removed() bool`.
- `localReader.reopen(ctx, failed)` now takes the failed source, closes it via `defer failed.Close()`, and after reopen completes, reports a gap if the interface had left the failed source's namespace (`failed.Removed()`).
- `afpacketSource` gains a "probe" socket: a raw AF_PACKET socket bound to the interface with protocol 0 (no packet handler). `Removed()` checks via `Getsockname` whether the bound `Ifindex` has been reset to -1 by the kernel (interface removed from namespace / deleted / moved), vs. `s.ifindex` recorded at open.
